<div id="A6" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix F </span>Prompt Design and Output Protocol

<div id="A6.p1" class="ltx_para">

The current prompt style is <span id="A6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">ranked_zscore</span>. This format is used to make the LLM output directly usable as a candidate-level ranking signal. The prompt consists of a system instruction that specifies the output contract and a user instruction that provides the query, optional candidate-set synopsis, and candidate CVE list.

</div>

<div id="A6.p2" class="ltx_para">

The system prompt follows the structure below:

> You are a CVE prioritization expert. Output ONLY a <span id="A6.p2.2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">\[Scores\]</span> section. Use one line per candidate in the format <span id="A6.p2.2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">CVE-ID: -0.00</span>. Scores are normalized within this candidate set and may be signed values. Sort all lines from highest score to lowest score. Include all candidates exactly once.

</div>

<div id="A6.p3" class="ltx_para">

The user prompt contains the following fields:

> <span id="A6.p3.2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">Question: \<query\>  
> Candidate-set synopsis: \<sample_summary\></span>  
> <span id="A6.p3.2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">CVE candidates:  
> \[1\] CVE-...  
> \[2\] CVE-...  
> ...  
> Output \[Scores\] now (sorted highest to lowest):</span>

</div>

<div id="A6.p4" class="ltx_para">

During data generation for policy training, the same prompt format is paired with a target answer in the <span id="A6.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">\[Scores\]</span> block. The target scores come from the constructed policy-specific ranking supervision, standardized within the candidate set, and sorted from highest to lowest. During inference, the model is required to generate the same <span id="A6.p4.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">\[Scores\]</span> format. The generated output is parsed by extracting explicit CVE-score pairs. If the output does not contain sufficient valid candidate scores, the system retries generation up to five times. Samples that still fail the output contract are marked invalid.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A7" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix G </span>Preference Scenarios and Policy Definitions

<div id="A7.p1" class="ltx_para">

This appendix clarifies the difference between *policies* used for adapter training and *preference scenarios* used for testing. A *policy* defines a view-specific scoring preference; the corresponding ranking targets are used only as supervision for adapter training. A *preference scenario* defines a prioritization objective under which ground-truth rankings are annotated by cybersecurity practitioners. The preference scenarios and their annotation guidelines were developed independently of the policy templates used for adapter training and do not instruct annotators to reproduce any policy weighting scheme. Instead, they define qualitative prioritization objectives based on the available vulnerability evidence rather than predefined feature weights, scoring formulas, or policy-specific ranking rules. At test time, the scoring prompt does not include a preference-scenario ID; fusion instead uses the support bank $B_{\gamma}$ attached to the evaluation run (Appendix <a href="#A2" class="ltx_ref" title="Appendix B Support-Bank Protocol ‣ HARP: Hierarchical Adaptive Ranking with Preference-Adaptive Fusion for Query-Based CVE Prioritization"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">B</span></a>).

</div>

<div id="A7.p2" class="ltx_para">

We train the scoring modules with policy-specific supervision rather than directly using support-bank labels because the policies capture relatively stable and reusable prioritization behaviors, whereas preference scenarios may vary across organizations and over time. This separation makes HARP a general framework: trained policy adapters can be reused across scenarios, while adaptation to a new or changing scenario requires only updating the support bank and refitting the fusion weights, rather than retraining the scoring modules. Although the policy set can also be extended when needed, it is expected to change less frequently than deployment-specific preference scenarios, making the overall adaptation process more efficient.

</div>

<div id="A7.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">G.1 </span>Candidate Evidence

<div id="A7.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para">

For candidate scoring, the LLM scorer observes the natural-language query, the candidate set, the candidate-set synopsis, and candidate-level vulnerability evidence (CVE records, CVSS metadata, exploit signals, references, and product information). Separately, the fusion module observes a small sample of labeled supports from $B_{\gamma}$. The preference scenario is therefore not hidden from the full system: it enters through the support bank used for weight fitting, while remaining absent from the scorer prompt as an explicit tag.

</div>

<div id="A7.SS1.p2" class="ltx_para">

The candidate-level evidence includes textual description, CVSS severity label, CVSS base score, attack vector, exploit status, exploit flag, CWE identifiers, affected product keys, platform information, patch signals from references, reference count, version-related signals, and publication recency. These fields are summarized into the candidate feature summary provided to the LLM. For policy-adapter training, they are also converted into numerical feature contributions that guide the construction of policy-specific ranking supervision.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A7.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">G.2 </span>Feature Contribution Functions

<div id="A7.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para">

We first define a common set of feature contribution functions $\{{\phi_{f}\hspace{0pt}{(c)}}\}$ for each candidate CVE $c$. These functions map raw vulnerability evidence to normalized numerical values and are used only to guide the construction of policy-specific training targets. Table <a href="#A7.T4" class="ltx_ref" title="Table 4 ‣ G.2 Feature Contribution Functions ‣ Appendix G Preference Scenarios and Policy Definitions ‣ HARP: Hierarchical Adaptive Ranking with Preference-Adaptive Fusion for Query-Based CVE Prioritization"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> lists the feature contribution rules used in our implementation.

</div>

<figure id="A7.T4" class="ltx_table">
<table id="A7.T4.2" class="ltx_tabular ltx_align_middle">
<tbody>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.1" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Feature</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.1.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_tt" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.1.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Contribution Rule</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.2" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.2.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_t" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">exploit</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.2.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_t" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">1.0</span> if <span id="A7.T4.2.2.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">KNOWN_EXPLOITED</span>; <span class="math inline">0.6</span> if <span id="A7.T4.2.2.2.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">has_exploit</span> is true; otherwise <span class="math inline">0.0</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.3" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.3.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">severity</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.3.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt">If <span id="A7.T4.2.3.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">base_score</span> exists, <span class="math inline">min (1, base_score/10)</span>; otherwise use severity mapping</td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.4" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.4.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">patch</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.4.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">1.0</span> if <span id="A7.T4.2.4.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">has_patch_in_references</span> is true; otherwise <span class="math inline">0.0</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.5" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.5.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.5.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">attack_vector</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.5.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt">NETWORK: <span class="math inline">1.0</span>; ADJACENT: <span class="math inline">0.5</span>; LOCAL: <span class="math inline">0.2</span>; PHYSICAL: <span class="math inline">0.1</span>; otherwise <span class="math inline">0.0</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.6" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.6.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.6.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">recency</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.6.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">max (0, 1 − days_since_published/365)</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.7" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.7.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.7.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">version_info</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.7.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">0.5 ⋅ has_version_info + 0.5 ⋅ affects_legacy_versions</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.8" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.8.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.8.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">reference</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.8.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">min (1, reference_count/10)</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.9" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.9.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.9.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">cwe</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.9.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">min (1, len(cwe_ids)/5)</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.10" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.10.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.10.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">platform</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.10.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">1.0</span> if platform list is non-empty; otherwise <span class="math inline">0.0</span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T4.2.11" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T4.2.11.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_bb" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span id="A7.T4.2.11.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">scope</span></td>
<td id="A7.T4.2.11.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_border_bb" style="padding-left: 2.0pt; padding-right: 2.0pt"><span class="math inline">min (1, len(product_keys)/10)</span></td>
</tr>

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table">Table 5: </span>Policy weights of the three views <em>global</em>, <em>enterprise</em> and <em>user</em>.</figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="A7.SS2.p2" class="ltx_para">

For textual severity labels, we use the following mapping:

|     |            |                       |     |                                                                         |
|-----|------------|-----------------------|-----|-------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|     | $CRITICAL$ | $= 1.0,{HIGH} = 0.7,$ |     | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equationgroup ltx_align_right">(25)</span> |
|     | $MEDIUM$   | $= 0.3,{LOW} = 0.1.$  |     |                                                                         |

All other severity labels are mapped to $0.0$.

</div>

<div id="A7.SS2.p3" class="ltx_para">

Given any feature-weight vector $\lambda$, we first clip negative feature weights and normalize the remaining positive weights:

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                             |     |                                                                    |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------|
|     | $${{{\overset{\sim}{\lambda}}_{f} = {\max{(\lambda_{f},0)}}},{{\overline{\lambda}}_{f} = \frac{{\overset{\sim}{\lambda}}_{f}}{\sum_{f^{\prime}}{\overset{\sim}{\lambda}}_{f^{\prime}}}}}.$$ |     | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(26)</span> |

We then compute a generic weighted feature score:

|     |                                                                                                                                                 |     |                                                                    |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------|
|     | $${{s_{\lambda}\hspace{0pt}{(c)}} = {\max\left( 0,{\sum\limits_{f}{{\overline{\lambda}}_{f}\hspace{0pt}\phi_{f}\hspace{0pt}{(c)}}} \right)}}.$$ |     | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(27)</span> |

This equation is a generic scoring template used only when constructing policy-specific training targets.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A7.SS3" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">G.3 </span>Policy Training Targets

<div id="A7.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para">

A policy is a discrete scoring preference template used to train a policy-specific adapter. Policies are organized under the three prioritization views: *global*, *enterprise*, and *user*. Each policy $\pi_{l,r}$ in view $l$ is associated with a feature-weight template $\lambda_{l,r}$ that guides the construction of its ranking supervision. The policy-specific training score for candidate $c$ is defined as

|     |                                                                                |     |                                                                    |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------|
|     | $${{y_{l,r}^{pol}\hspace{0pt}{(c)}} = {s_{\lambda_{l,r}}\hspace{0pt}{(c)}}}.$$ |     | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(28)</span> |

The ranking induced by $y_{l,r}^{pol}\hspace{0pt}{(c)}$ is used only to train the adapter corresponding to policy $\pi_{l,r}$. These scores are not the evaluation labels used in the final benchmark, and the policy templates are never applied to construct test rankings.

</div>

<div id="A7.SS3.p2" class="ltx_para">

Different policies emphasize different remediation criteria over the same candidate evidence. For example, an exploit-focused policy assigns larger weights to exploit-related features, while a patch-focused policy assigns larger weights to patch-related features. The feature-weight templates used to guide construction of all policy adapters are listed in Table <a href="#A7.T5" class="ltx_ref" title="Table 5 ‣ G.2 Feature Contribution Functions ‣ Appendix G Preference Scenarios and Policy Definitions ‣ HARP: Hierarchical Adaptive Ranking with Preference-Adaptive Fusion for Query-Based CVE Prioritization"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A7.SS4" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">G.4 </span>Scenario-Based Test Annotation

<div id="A7.SS4.p1" class="ltx_para">

Preference scenarios define the evaluation objectives: *importance*, *exploit_first*, and *patch_order*. For each scenario, ground-truth rankings were annotated by cybersecurity practitioners from our organization’s IT/security team using the query, candidate evidence, and a scenario-specific prioritization guideline. The same query–candidate instances can therefore receive different rankings under different scenarios. Annotators independently assessed the candidate CVEs according to their professional judgment, and remaining disagreements were resolved through discussion to obtain a consensus ranking. The policy feature-weight templates used for adapter training were not available to the annotators and were never used to generate evaluation labels. Instead, these templates served solely to construct policy-specific supervision for training the adapters. These test rankings are independent of the policy-training templates. The templates provide supervision only for adapter training, whereas the evaluation scenarios define qualitative human annotation guidelines. Although both are motivated by common cybersecurity remediation objectives, the evaluation scenarios were designed independently and are not derived from the policy templates.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A8" class="section ltx_appendix">

</div>

<div id="A9" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix I </span>Sensitivity Analysis on $\alpha$

<div id="A9.p1" class="ltx_para">

We further study the effect of the interpolation coefficient $\alpha$ in the final score ${F{(q,c_{i})}} = {{\alpha\hspace{0pt}F_{adapt}\hspace{0pt}{(q,c_{i})}} + {{({1 - \alpha})}\hspace{0pt}F_{uni}\hspace{0pt}{(q,c_{i})}}}$. Figure <a href="#A9.F3" class="ltx_ref" title="Figure 3 ‣ Appendix I Sensitivity Analysis on 𝛼 ‣ HARP: Hierarchical Adaptive Ranking with Preference-Adaptive Fusion for Query-Based CVE Prioritization"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> reports the sensitivity results on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct across the three preference scenarios. The dashed vertical line marks the value used in our main experiments, $\alpha = 0.3$.

</div>

<figure id="A9.F3" class="ltx_figure">

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure">Figure 3: </span>Sensitivity analysis of the interpolation coefficient <span class="math inline"><em>α</em></span> on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct. We report Precision@K, MAP@K, and MRR@K under the importance, exploit-first, and patch-order preference scenarios. The dashed vertical line indicates the value used in the main experiments, <span class="math inline"><em>α</em> = 0.3</span>.</figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="A9.p2" class="ltx_para">

The effect of $\alpha$ differs across preference scenarios. In the *importance* preference scenario, all three metrics increase slightly when $\alpha$ grows from $0$ to $0.3$, and then decrease as $\alpha$ becomes larger. This suggests that a moderate amount of adaptive fusion improves overall-priority ranking, while relying too heavily on adaptive fusion weakens the stabilizing effect of the uniform multi-view term. In the *exploit-first* preference scenario, the curves are more non-monotonic, but the best or near-best results still appear around $\alpha = 0.3$ for MRR@K and Precision@K, with MAP@K remaining competitive. This indicates that exploit-focused ranking benefits from adaptive view weighting, but the improvement is not strictly monotonic.

</div>

<div id="A9.p3" class="ltx_para">

The *patch-order* preference scenario shows a different trend. Performance is strongest when $\alpha$ is small and generally decreases as $\alpha$ increases. This suggests that patch-oriented ranking benefits more from preserving balanced evidence across views, and excessive adaptive weighting may overemphasize one view at the cost of other useful patch-related signals. Overall, $\alpha$ trades off support-conditioned adaptive fusion against uniform multi-view stability. We select $\alpha = 0.3$ on the development split as a robust operating point across preference scenarios; the optimum can still vary by objective (e.g., patch-order favors smaller $\alpha$).

</div>

</div>

<div id="A10" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix J </span>Case Studies

<div id="A10.p1" class="ltx_para">

We present two cases under Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct with $\alpha = 0.3$ to examine how HARP combines view-specific rankings. Each case reports the ranking question, a brief candidate-set synopsis, the ground-truth ranking, and the rankings produced by the global, enterprise, user, and fusion scores. We focus on top-$K$ behavior: which candidate each view places first, how the fused ranking differs from the single-view rankings, and what the fitted view weights imply about relative view contributions under the selected policy configuration.

</div>

<div id="A10.p2" class="ltx_para">

The case boxes below follow the same structure. A check mark indicates that the corresponding view or fusion result ranks the ground-truth top candidate at rank 1, while a cross indicates otherwise.

</div>

<div id="A11" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix K </span>Use of AI Assistance

<div id="A11.p1" class="ltx_para">

We used ChatGPT only as a writing assistance tool to improve the clarity, grammar, and readability of the manuscript. The tool was not used to generate research ideas, design the method, conduct experiments, produce experimental results, or draw scientific conclusions. All technical content, experimental design, implementation, analysis, and final claims were created and verified by the authors.

## Instructions for reporting errors

We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the methods listed below:

- Click the "Report Issue" <span class="mobile-only">(![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyByb2xlPSJwcmVzZW50YXRpb24iIHN0eWxlPSJkaXNwbGF5OiBpbmxpbmUtYmxvY2s7IHZlcnRpY2FsLWFsaWduOiBtaWRkbGU7IGZpbGw6IHZhcigtLXRleHQtY29sb3IpOyIgYXJpYS1oaWRkZW49InRydWUiIGhlaWdodD0iMWVtIiB2aWV3Ym94PSIwIDAgNjQwIDY0MCI+CiAgICAgICAgICAgIDxwYXRoIGQ9Ik0yMjQgMTYwQzIyNCAxMDcgMjY3IDY0IDMyMCA2NEMzNzMgNjQgNDE2IDEwNyA0MTYgMTYwTDQxNiAxNjMuNkM0MTYgMTc5LjMgNDAzLjMgMTkyIDM4Ny42IDE5MkwyNTIuNSAxOTJDMjM2LjggMTkyIDIyNC4xIDE3OS4zIDIyNC4xIDE2My42TDIyNC4xIDE2MHpNNTY5LjYgMTcyLjhDNTgwLjIgMTg2LjkgNTc3LjMgMjA3IDU2My4yIDIxNy42TDQ2NS40IDI5MC45QzQ3MC43IDI5OS44IDQ3NC43IDMwOS42IDQ3Ny4yIDMyMEw1NzYgMzIwQzU5My43IDMyMCA2MDggMzM0LjMgNjA4IDM1MkM2MDggMzY5LjcgNTkzLjcgMzg0IDU3NiAzODRMNDgwIDM4NEw0ODAgNDE2QzQ4MCA0MTguNiA0NzkuOSA0MjEuMyA0NzkuOCA0MjMuOUw1NjMuMiA0ODYuNEM1NzcuMyA0OTcgNTgwLjIgNTE3LjEgNTY5LjYgNTMxLjJDNTU5IDU0NS4zIDUzOC45IDU0OC4yIDUyNC44IDUzNy42TDQ2MS43IDQ5MC4zQzQzOC41IDUzNC41IDM5NS4yIDU2Ni41IDM0NCA1NzQuMkwzNDQgMzQ0QzM0NCAzMzAuNyAzMzMuMyAzMjAgMzIwIDMyMEMzMDYuNyAzMjAgMjk2IDMzMC43IDI5NiAzNDRMMjk2IDU3NC4yQzI0NC44IDU2Ni41IDIwMS41IDUzNC41IDE3OC4zIDQ5MC4zTDExNS4yIDUzNy42QzEwMS4xIDU0OC4yIDgxIDU0NS4zIDcwLjQgNTMxLjJDNTkuOCA1MTcuMSA2Mi43IDQ5NyA3Ni44IDQ4Ni40TDE2MC4yIDQyMy45QzE2MC4xIDQyMS4zIDE2MCA0MTguNyAxNjAgNDE2TDE2MCAzODRMNjQgMzg0QzQ2LjMgMzg0IDMyIDM2OS43IDMyIDM1MkMzMiAzMzQuMyA0Ni4zIDMyMCA2NCAzMjBMMTYyLjggMzIwQzE2NS4zIDMwOS42IDE2OS4zIDI5OS44IDE3NC42IDI5MC45TDc2LjggMjE3LjZDNjIuNyAyMDcgNTkuOCAxODYuOSA3MC40IDE3Mi44QzgxIDE1OC43IDEwMS4xIDE1NS44IDExNS4yIDE2Ni40TDIyNCAyNDhDMjM2LjMgMjQyLjkgMjQ5LjggMjQwIDI2NCAyNDBMMzc2IDI0MEMzOTAuMiAyNDAgNDAzLjcgMjQyLjggNDE2IDI0OEw1MjQuOCAxNjYuNEM1MzguOSAxNTUuOCA1NTkgMTU4LjcgNTY5LjYgMTcyLjh6IiAvPgogICAgICAgICAgPC9zdmc+))</span> button, located in the page header.

**Tip:** You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.

Our team has already identified <a href="https://github.com/arXiv/html_feedback/issues" class="ltx_ref" target="_blank">the following issues</a>. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for all.

Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a <a href="https://github.com/brucemiller/LaTeXML/wiki/Porting-LaTeX-packages-for-LaTeXML" class="ltx_ref" target="_blank">list of packages that need conversion</a>, and welcome <a href="https://github.com/brucemiller/LaTeXML/issues" class="ltx_ref" target="_blank">developer contributions</a>.
